[GL-0001] Bill of lading (B/L)
A sea freight document issued by the ocean carrier. It is evidence that cargo was received and of the terms of the shipping contract.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0002] Airway bill (AWB)
The air cargo document issued by the airline or forwarder for a consignment. Unlike a bill of lading, it is not a title document.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0003] Bin
A numbered storage location in the warehouse, recorded in the WMS.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0004] Bulk cargo
Goods shipped loose rather than in packages, such as grain or coal.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0005] Carrier
The company that physically moves the goods, such as a road transporter, airline or shipping line.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0006] Carton
A corrugated cardboard box used as the basic outer pack for a shipment.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0007] Chargeable weight
The higher of actual weight and volumetric weight, used to price a shipment.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0008] Consignee
The person or company who receives the goods.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0009] Consignor
The person or company who sends the goods. Also called the shipper.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0010] Container
A standard steel box used for sea and rail freight, usually 20 or 40 feet long.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0011] Cross-docking
Moving goods straight from inbound to outbound with little or no storage in between.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0012] Customs broker
A licensed agent who files customs declarations for an importer or exporter.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0013] Cycle count
A partial stock count done on a rolling schedule, instead of one full stock take.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0014] Dead stock
Inventory that has not moved for a long time and is unlikely to sell.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0015] Demurrage
A charge for leaving a container at a port or terminal beyond the free time.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0016] Detention
A charge for keeping a container outside the terminal beyond the free time.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0017] Dimensional weight
Another name for volumetric weight, calculated from the length, width and height of a shipment.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0018] Dispatch
Sending a shipment out of the warehouse together with its documents.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0019] Dunnage
Packing material, such as wood or inflatable bags, that stops cargo moving inside a container or vehicle.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0020] Incoterms
Standard international trade terms that set who pays for transport and who carries the risk at each stage of a sale.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0021] EXW (Ex Works)
The buyer collects the goods from the seller's premises and takes on most of the cost and risk.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0022] FOB (Free on Board)
The seller delivers the goods on board the vessel at the port of shipment.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0023] CIF (Cost, Insurance and Freight)
The seller pays the cost, insurance and freight to the named destination port.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0024] DAP (Delivered at Place)
The seller delivers the goods ready for unloading at the named place. The buyer handles import clearance.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0025] DDP (Delivered Duty Paid)
The seller delivers the goods cleared for import, with all duties paid.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0026] Forwarder
A company that arranges transport and paperwork for shipments, often using several carriers.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0027] Freight
The charge for moving goods, or the goods being moved, depending on the context.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0028] Goods receipt note (GRN)
A record made at receiving that confirms goods arrived and were checked.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0029] GST (Goods and Services Tax)
India's indirect tax on the supply of goods and services.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0030] GSTIN
The 15-character GST identification number of a registered business.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0031] HSN code
A standard code that classifies goods for GST purposes.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0032] HS code
The Harmonized System code used internationally to classify goods for customs duty.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0033] Hub
A consolidation or distribution point where shipments are sorted and transferred.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0034] ICD (Inland Container Depot)
A container terminal located inland, where containers are stored, cleared and transferred.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0035] Inbound
Goods coming into a warehouse or location.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0036] Inventory
The stock of goods a business holds.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0037] Last mile
The final leg of delivery, from a local hub to the consignee.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0038] Load securing
Methods such as straps, blocking and bracing that stop cargo moving during transit.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0039] Lot number
A code that groups items made or received together, used for traceability.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0040] Manifest
A list of all the shipments or items on one vehicle, flight or vessel.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0041] Mode of transport
The method used to move goods, such as road, rail, air, sea or a combination.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0042] Outbound
Goods leaving a warehouse or location.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0043] Pallet
A flat platform, usually wood or plastic, used to move and stack goods with a forklift.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0044] Pick list
The list of items and bin locations a picker follows to gather an order.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0045] Put-away
Moving received goods into their storage bins and recording the location.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0046] Quarantine
A separate area for goods on hold, such as damaged or unverified stock.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0047] Reason code
A short code that explains why a shipment has an exception, such as a hold or delay.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0048] Reverse logistics
Moving goods back from the consignee for return, repair or disposal.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0049] Route
The planned sequence of hubs and legs that a shipment follows.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0050] Safety stock
Extra stock kept to cover unexpected demand or late supply.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0051] SKU (stock keeping unit)
A unique code for each distinct product variant held in stock.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0052] Shrinkage
Stock lost from inventory through damage, theft or counting errors.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0053] Slotting
Assigning products to storage locations, usually based on how fast they move.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0054] Stock take
A full physical count of all inventory in a warehouse.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0055] Tare weight
The weight of an empty container, vehicle or packaging.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0056] Gross weight
The total weight of the goods plus their packaging.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0057] Net weight
The weight of the goods alone, without packaging.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0058] Transshipment
Moving cargo from one vehicle or vessel to another on the way to its destination.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0059] Unit load
Goods combined into one handling unit, such as a palletised load or a shrink-wrapped stack.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0060] Volumetric weight
A weight calculated from the size of a shipment. It applies when a shipment is bulky but light.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0061] Warehouse Management System (WMS)
Software that tracks stock locations, movements and orders inside a warehouse.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0062] Zone
A defined area of the warehouse, such as fast-moving stock or hazardous storage.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0063] Cold chain
A chain of temperature-controlled storage and transport that keeps goods within the required range.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0064] Temperature log
A record of temperature readings for goods in storage or in transit.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0065] Hazmat
Short for hazardous materials: goods that can pose a risk to people, property or the environment.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0066] UN number
A four-digit code that identifies a dangerous good for transport.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0067] Fragile mark
A label that tells handlers the carton contains breakable goods.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0068] Pilferage
Theft of small quantities of goods from a shipment or warehouse.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0069] Customs clearance
The process by which customs releases goods after declarations are filed and duties are settled.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0070] Lead time
The time between placing an order and receiving the goods.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0071] Expiry date
The date after which a product should no longer be used or sold.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0072] Shelf life
How long a product stays fit for use or sale.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0073] Serial number
A unique number given to an individual item, used for tracking and warranty.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0074] Batch
A group of items produced together under the same conditions.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0075] Backorder
An order that cannot be filled from current stock and waits for replenishment.
Tags: glossary;term

[GL-0076] Consolidation
Combining several small shipments into one larger load to reduce cost.
Tags: glossary;term

[WH-0001] Receiving dock check
Every inbound vehicle is checked at the dock against the ASN before unloading. Seals are verified and noted on the receiving record.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0002] Put-away after receiving
After receiving, goods are moved to their bin, and the bin location is scanned into the WMS.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0003] Daily cycle counting
A small set of bins is counted every day on a rotating schedule, so differences are found early without stopping operations.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0004] Monthly stock reconciliation
System stock is compared with physical counts each month. Differences are investigated before any adjustment is posted.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0005] Quarantine handling
Damaged or unverified goods go to a separate quarantine area and are not picked until the Warehouse Manager releases them.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0006] Returns receiving
Returned goods are inspected and recorded with their return reason. They are then restocked, repaired or moved to quarantine.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0007] Cross-docking at the dock
Goods that already have a dispatch order move from the dock straight to the outbound bay, with little or no storage.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0008] Loading sequence
Cartons are loaded in reverse delivery order, so the first delivery is accessible when the vehicle is opened.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0009] Load securing before departure
Cargo is strapped or blocked before the vehicle leaves, and the securing is checked at the gate.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0010] Pallet build standard
Pallets are built square, with heavy items at the bottom and the stack kept within the height and weight limits of the goods.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0011] Stacking limits
The printed stacking limit on each carton is respected. Cartons are never stacked on top of fragile goods.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0012] Forklift safety
Only trained operators use forklifts. Pedestrians stay out of marked lanes, and loads are kept low while travelling.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0013] Personal protective equipment
Staff wear safety shoes and gloves on the dock, and high-visibility vests in the yard.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0014] Fire exits and extinguishers
Fire exits and extinguisher points are kept clear at all times. Blocked exits are reported to the Warehouse Manager at once.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0015] Housekeeping
Aisles are cleared at the end of each shift, and spilled liquids are cleaned up immediately to prevent slips.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0016] Pest control
Regular inspection and treatment reduce rodent and insect risk. Records are kept, especially for food and grain stock.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0017] Cold room temperature logging
Temperatures in the cold area are logged at set intervals. Any reading outside the required range is escalated at once.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0018] Dock door scheduling
Inbound and outbound vehicles are assigned dock doors so that loading and unloading do not overlap.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0019] Gate check-in
Every vehicle is logged at the gate with its number and driver details. Gate passes are issued where needed.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0020] Vehicle inspection before loading
The loading bay checks the vehicle for cleanliness, damage and fitness to carry the goods before loading starts.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0021] Seal verification
Seal numbers are checked against the documents at loading and at receiving. A broken or mismatched seal is reported at once.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0022] Container stuffing record
Each container load is recorded with its seal number, weight and carton count, and photographed where required.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0023] Labelling at packing
Each carton gets a label with the LR number, carton number and destination before it leaves the packing station.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0024] Barcode scanning at pick and dispatch
Every carton and pallet is scanned at pick, pack and dispatch, so the system matches what was actually moved.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0025] Serial number capture
Serialised products are scanned at receiving and dispatch, so each unit can be traced.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0026] Batch capture at receiving
Batch and expiry details are captured at receiving and linked to each bin, so that recalls can be traced.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0027] Near-expiry monitoring
A weekly report lists stock close to expiry, so it can be dispatched or flagged before it becomes dead stock.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0028] Dead stock review
Items that have not moved for a long time are reviewed with the client to decide on return, discount or disposal.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0029] Safety stock settings
Each client's safety stock level is set in the WMS. The Warehouse Manager is alerted when stock falls below it.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0030] Replenishment from reserve
Forward pick bins are topped up from reserve storage before they run out, so pickers are not held up.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0031] Slotting by movement speed
Fast-moving products are placed near the dispatch bays and slow movers further back, to reduce travel time.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0032] Order consolidation
Orders for the same consignee or route are combined where possible, to reduce the number of vehicles and cartons.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0033] Packing material control
Cartons, tape and dunnage are issued against orders, and stock is counted monthly to control wastage.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0034] Damage recording
Damage found during handling is photographed and recorded in the WMS with a damage code. The Warehouse Manager is informed.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0035] Weighing and dimension capture
Each carton's weight and dimensions are recorded at packing. This supports billing and the chargeable weight calculation.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0036] Shrinkage investigation
Unexplained shortages are investigated by comparing scan logs, CCTV footage where available, and count records.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0037] Shift handover
Each shift ends with a handover note covering open exceptions, pending dispatches and any hazards.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0038] Incident reporting
Accidents, near misses and spills are reported on the same day, with the time, location and people involved.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0039] Access control
Only authorised staff and visitors enter the storage area. Visitors sign in and wear visitor badges.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0040] Hazmat segregation
Hazardous goods are stored only in Zone H, and incompatible materials are kept apart as set in the storage plan.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0041] Spill kits
Spill kits are placed near chemical and liquid storage, and staff are trained to use them.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0042] Monsoon preparation
Before the monsoon, roofs, drains and low-lying areas are checked, and stock is moved off the floor where water can reach it.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0043] Festival season planning
Peak periods before festivals are planned in advance, with extra staff, dock slots and safety stock.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0044] Dispatch confirmation
A dispatch is confirmed only after the documents, weights and carton counts match the order, and the status is updated.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0045] Pick accuracy checks
Random picks are re-checked each day, and any pick error is traced to the picker and the bin label.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[WH-0046] Two-person check for high-value goods
High-value consignments are counted and sealed by two people, and the sealing is recorded.
Tags: warehouse;operations

[CL-0001] Claim for outer carton crushed on electronics
Note the crushed carton on the POD at the door, photograph all sides, and keep the carton until the claim is closed. For electronics: Keep the serial number and original packaging with the claim. Claims must be filed within 7 days of delivery with photos and the invoice (see PO-001 and FAQ-003). Carrier liability is limited to the declared value unless extended cover was bought at booking.
Tags: claim;damage;electronics

[PL-0001] Booking cancellation
To cancel a booking, contact the Logistics Coordinator in writing. Cancellation terms are set out in the client agreement. A booking that has already been picked up is handled as a return or re-routing request.
Tags: policy;booking cancellation

[PL-0002] Pickup rescheduling
The sender can reschedule a pickup through the Logistics Coordinator. The new date is confirmed before the carrier is reassigned.
Tags: policy;pickup rescheduling

[PL-0003] Failed pickup
If the sender is not ready at the pickup time, the attempt is logged as Pickup Attempted and a new pickup is scheduled.
Tags: policy;failed pickup

[PL-0004] Weight and volume disputes
If the weighed weight differs from the booked weight, the Logistics Coordinator reviews the weighment slip and the packing list before the bill is finalised.
Tags: policy;weight and volume disputes

[PL-0005] Invoice queries
Billing queries must be raised in writing with the invoice number. Disputes not resolved at Level 1 move to the Operations Manager (see PO-004).
Tags: policy;invoice queries

[PL-0006] Payment terms
Payment terms are set in the client agreement. Check the agreement, or contact your account manager, for the terms that apply to you.
Tags: policy;payment terms

[PL-0007] Extended cover at booking
Extended insurance cover must be selected at booking. Goods without extended cover are protected only up to the carrier liability limit (see PO-001).
Tags: policy;extended cover at booking

[PL-0008] Prohibited and restricted goods
Goods that are illegal to transport, or that the carrier does not accept, cannot be booked. Ask the Logistics Coordinator before booking any unusual item.
Tags: policy;prohibited and restricted goods

[PL-0009] Hazardous goods rules
Hazardous goods move only with a carrier holding a valid hazmat certificate, and they are stored only in Zone H (see SP-002 and WO-002).
Tags: policy;hazardous goods rules

[PL-0010] Customs and declarations
The client is responsible for the accuracy of invoices, HS codes and declared values on export and import documents.
Tags: policy;customs and declarations

[PL-0011] Force majeure
Delays caused by events outside FleetHarbor's control, such as natural disasters, strikes or government action, are not treated as service failures. The status and reason code are updated as events happen.
Tags: policy;force majeure

[PL-0012] Holiday schedule
Deliveries run Monday to Saturday between 9:00 AM and 7:00 PM. Public holidays can change the schedule, and the Logistics Coordinator confirms revised dates.
Tags: policy;holiday schedule

[PL-0013] Complaint handling
Complaints start with the Logistics Coordinator for the lane. Unresolved complaints move to the Operations Manager and then to the Head of Operations (see PO-004).
Tags: policy;complaint handling

[PL-0014] Consignee refusal
If the consignee refuses delivery, the shipment is logged as a Failed Attempt with the refusal reason, and the sender is contacted for the next step.
Tags: policy;consignee refusal

[PL-0015] Data access requests
Clients can request records of their own shipments through the Logistics Coordinator. Consignee data is handled as described in the data and privacy policy (see PO-003).
Tags: policy;data access requests

[PL-0016] Document retention
Shipping documents are kept in the system for the period set in the client agreement and as required by applicable law.
Tags: policy;document retention

[PL-0017] Packaging responsibility
The client is responsible for packaging that suits the goods and the route. FleetHarbor may refuse packaging that appears unsafe and will record the reason.
Tags: policy;packaging responsibility

[PL-0018] Client portal access
Each client user has a named login for the client portal. Access should be shared only with users the client has authorised.
Tags: policy;client portal access

[PL-0019] Contact changes
The client must tell FleetHarbor in writing when a pickup or consignee contact changes, so that the updated details are used for dispatch and delivery.
Tags: policy;contact changes

[PL-0020] Escalation of priority shipments
A priority shipment moves to the next escalation level if it has no resolution within 4 hours (see PO-004 and CO-002).
Tags: policy;escalation of priority shipments

[PL-0021] Support hours
Support is provided through the Logistics Coordinator during working hours. Priority issues follow the escalation matrix.
Tags: policy;support hours

[FQ-0001] How do I know my order has shipped?
Check the tracking page with your order ID or LR number. The status changes to In Transit once the carrier has picked up the shipment (see SS-002).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0002] Can I ask for a different delivery time?
Deliveries run from 9:00 AM to 7:00 PM, Monday to Saturday. Ask the Logistics Coordinator for your lane to request a time within these hours. Any delivery outside them needs written approval from the Operations Manager and the client.
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0003] What is an LR number?
The LR number is the lorry receipt number generated at booking. It is shared with the carrier, so use it to track the shipment after booking (see TM-001).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0004] Why does my order show On Hold?
A hold means an exception was logged with a reason code, such as missing documents, weather or capacity limits. The order is not cancelled, and the reason code is shown on the tracking page.
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0005] Can I change my order after it is dispatched?
After dispatch, changes must be requested through the carrier, and a re-routing fee may apply. Ask the Logistics Coordinator for your lane as soon as possible.
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0006] How many delivery attempts will be made?
Up to three delivery attempts are made. After the third failed attempt, the shipment returns to the origin hub (see DP-001).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0007] Do I need to sign for my delivery?
Proof of delivery needs the receiver's name, a signature or OTP confirmation, and a timestamp (see DP-002).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0008] Can I send goods without an invoice?
No. Dispatch is blocked in the system until the mandatory documents, including the invoice and packing list, are attached (see SP-003).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0009] Do you deliver on Sundays?
Standard deliveries run Monday to Saturday. Any Sunday or out-of-hours delivery needs written approval from the Operations Manager and the client (see DP-003).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0010] Why was my shipment returned?
A shipment is returned after three failed delivery attempts, or when a return is agreed. Its status then changes to Returned (see SS-005).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0011] How long do I have to return goods?
Returns are accepted within 10 days of delivery for unused goods in their original packaging (see PO-002).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0012] Who pays for return freight?
Return freight is charged to the client unless the return results from a FleetHarbor error (see PO-002).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0013] How much is my shipment insured for?
Carrier liability is limited to the declared value of the goods, unless extended cover was bought at booking (see PO-001).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0014] Is my contact information shared with the carrier?
Consignee details are used only for delivery. They are removed from active systems 90 days after proof of delivery and are not shared with third parties (see PO-003).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0015] Why is my tracking not updating?
In-transit shipments refresh every 30 minutes. If the status has not changed in 24 hours, contact the Logistics Coordinator for your lane (see FAQ-001 and OT-001).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0016] What is the difference between an order ID and an LR number?
Order IDs begin with ORD-. LR numbers begin with LR- and are shared with the carrier, so use the LR number to track after booking (see OT-002).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0017] How long does delivery take?
Standard windows are 2 to 4 business days for intra-state shipments and 4 to 7 business days for inter-state shipments (see CO-002).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0018] How do I mark a shipment as priority?
Priority shipments are flagged at booking. They receive daily status checks from the Logistics Coordinator (see CO-002).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0019] Who do I contact if my issue is not resolved?
Start with the Logistics Coordinator (Level 1). If the issue is still open, it goes to the Operations Manager (Level 2), then to the Head of Operations (Level 3). A priority shipment moves up after 4 hours without resolution (see PO-004).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0020] Do you handle hazardous goods?
Yes, but hazardous goods move only with a carrier that holds a valid hazmat certificate, and they are stored only in Zone H (see SP-002 and WO-002).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0021] Can you store my goods in your warehouse?
FleetHarbor offers warehousing from its Ahmedabad, Vadodara and Rajkot warehouses (see CO-001). Contact your account manager to arrange storage.
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0022] How are goods picked from the warehouse?
Perishable goods are picked FEFO, and all other goods are picked FIFO (see WO-003).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0023] What does Failed Attempt mean on my tracking?
Failed Attempt means a delivery was tried but not completed. Each attempt is recorded with a reason code (see DP-001 and SS-005).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0024] What happens if I refuse a delivery?
The refusal is logged as a Failed Attempt with its reason, and the sender is contacted for the next step.
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0025] How do I report missing cartons?
Compare the carton count with the packing list, note the shortage on the POD at delivery, and raise a claim within 7 days with photos and the invoice (see PO-001).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0026] Is my order confirmed as soon as I place it?
An order moves through Order Created and then Order Confirmed. The booking is made within 2 hours of confirmation (see LW-001 and SP-001).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0027] How do I track a shipment on the portal?
Log in to the client portal and enter your order ID or LR number on the tracking page. You can also call support to speak to a team member (see OT-001).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0028] What if I cannot receive the goods at the delivery address?
Tell the Logistics Coordinator the best time for the next attempt. Attempts are made only within delivery hours (see DP-003).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0029] What stages does an order go through?
Every order moves through six stages: Order Created, Order Confirmed, Picked, Packed, Dispatched and Delivered (see LW-001).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0030] Does an exception cancel my order?
No. An exception is logged as a hold with a reason code and does not cancel the order (see LW-001).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0031] What should I do if my tracking shows Delayed?
Check the reason code on the tracking page. Weather, missing documents and capacity limits are the usual reasons. Contact the Logistics Coordinator if there is no update within 24 hours (see SS-004).
Tags: faq;customer question

[FQ-0032] Who signs the POD for a delivery?
The receiver signs, or confirms by OTP, and the Delivery Executive records the receiver's name and a timestamp (see DP-002).
Tags: faq;customer question
